- [OTHER] **[sec][P2 · payment identity join — surfaced 2026-08-26 by the deploy car's 422 diagnosis, NOT fixed] `dodo_webhook` IGNORES the `data.metadata.clerk_user_id` every real payload carries and joins identity on `customer.email` → a LIVE Clerk lookup only — two runtime-state conditions make a REAL cancelled/expired/failed payload 422, one of them permanent and silent. (Also the standing answer to "why does Dodo's Testing-tab sample 422?" — BY DESIGN; do not re-diagnose.)**
  - **🔑 THE ONE-LINER FIRST, so nobody re-diagnoses the Dashboard:** **Dodo's Testing-tab `subscription.active` sample 422s BY DESIGN** — its `customer.email` is the fake `test@acme.com`, which has no Clerk account, so the live lookup returns `[]` and the handler answers `422 {"detail": "Clerk user not found"}` (`api/server.py:2150`), deterministically (both auto-retries identical; the ~1–3 s duration is the Clerk round-trip). This is a property of the synthetic sample, not a defect the sample found. Diagnosed + reproduced 2026-08-26: `tests/probes/dodo_422/` (11 scenarios, all matched, incl. the per-field mutation matrix proving `customer.email`-resolving-in-Clerk is the ONLY flip field — `metadata: {}` and `payment_method_id: null` are never read).
  - **The mechanism:** the route has NO Pydantic body model, so FastAPI's pre-handler 422 path does not exist; the only 422s are `:2128` ("No customer email") and `:2150` ("Clerk user not found"), both AFTER signature verification (unsigned garbage → 401, live-verified on fly 240). Identity is derived EXCLUSIVELY `customer.email` → `GET api.clerk.com/v1/users?email_address=…` (`:2131-2146`); the TEST_MODE bypass reads `data.clerk_user_id` / top-level / `customer.clerk_user_id` (`:2112-2117`) — **none of which real payloads have. The key real payloads DO have, `data.metadata.clerk_user_id` (stamped by our own checkout at `:1922`), is read NOWHERE in the handler.**
  - **Risk 1 — Clerk outage at delivery time (transient, retry-covered):** the lookup's `except` swallows any HTTP failure (`:2145-2146`), `clerk_user_id` stays `None`, and a REAL terminal event 422s. Probe scenario `real_shape_clerk_outage` reproduces it. Mitigated in practice: prod evidence shows Dodo auto-retries 422s — but an outage outlasting Dodo's retry schedule loses the downgrade silently.
  - **Risk 2 — subscriber removes the subscribe-time email from Clerk (permanent, silent):** checkout guarantees the Dodo customer email is the Clerk PRIMARY email at subscribe time (`:1900-1918`), and Clerk's `email_address` query matches ANY of the user's addresses — so a mere primary-email change does NOT break the join; **removal of the address does, permanently: every subsequent cancelled/expired/failed delivery 422s and the user keeps pro forever**, 200-free and invisible (the provider dashboard shows a delivery failure nobody watches). Narrow, but it is exactly the silent keep-pro shape the payment-coverage filing warned about.
  - **Hardening shape (small, NOT done here — deploy car was diagnose-and-ship-scoped):** read `data.metadata.clerk_user_id` FIRST (it is our own checkout's stamp riding a signature-verified payload), fall back to the email join; distinguish "Clerk answered empty" from "Clerk unreachable" so an outage returns 5xx (retryable-by-contract) instead of 422. Touches the production identity join for money events → wants its own car with the `test_payment_webhooks.py` fixtures extended, not a drive-by.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-08-26 (deploy-car Phase 0, verdict (a) BENIGN for the deploy gate). **Evidence:** `tests/probes/dodo_422/probe_dodo_sample_422.py` + result JSON (Rule 20).

- [OTHER] **[sec][P2 · data integrity / polymorphic key — surfaced 2026-08-18 during verification, NOT fixed] 🔴 `shared_query.query_id` holds TWO DIFFERENT KINDS OF ID depending on which button created the share, and a unique index spans both namespaces**
  - **The column is documented and generally used as the audit id** (`res_…` / `ver_…` / `exp_…`, minted at `api/server.py:788`/`1068`/`1549` and emitted to the client as `query_id`). **`ShareModal` posts whatever `queryId` prop it was handed** (`components/ShareModal.tsx:114` → `api/server.py:3166`), and there are exactly **two** `ShareButton` mount sites:
    - `components/Navbar.tsx:201` → `queryId={shareData.queryId}` = **the audit id**. ✅
    - 🔴 `pages/history.tsx:276` → **`queryId={String(item.id)}`**, where `item` is a `ChatHistory` ORM row from `GET /api/history` — so this stores **`chat_history.id`, the autoincrement INTEGER PK, stringified** (e.g. `"41"`).
  - **Consequence:** `shared_query.query_id` is a **polymorphic, untagged** column, and the partial unique index `uq_shared_query_query_creator ON shared_query(query_id, created_by) WHERE query_id IS NOT NULL` (`migrations/004_add_shared_query.sql`) is **enforced across both namespaces**. A decimal string and an audit id can never collide today only because the formats differ — nothing enforces that.
  - ⚠️ **Anyone who writes a join or a per-entry deletion against `query_id` will get it wrong for history-page shares**, which is precisely the population a history delete would touch. **Recorded here because the per-entry-deletion scoping in BACKLOG depends on it.**
  - **Related, same column family:** `bug_reports.query_id` **is** the audit id, but **(i)** `getCurrentQueryId()` reads a module global (`utils/analytics.ts:110-120`) that `pages/_app.tsx` never clears on route change, so a report filed on any other page carries the **stale** last-feature-page id; and **(ii)** for anonymous users the audit id is minted and emitted but **no `AuditLog` row is written** (`if not is_anonymous`), so the column can hold a well-formed id resolving to **zero** audit rows.
  - **Surfaced**: 2026-08-18 while verifying the claim that "`shared_query.query_id` and `bug_reports.query_id` both store the audit id" — half of which turned out to be false.

- [HONESTY] **[P2 · landing tagline / fourth unguarded surface — discovered by the 2026-08-11 landing recon; STAYS OPEN until the addendum review returns]** `landingContent.tagline` (`utils/i18n.ts`) carries the flagship provenance claim — *"Ask in your language. **Verified by official sources.** Answered in yours."* — in 16 machine-translated locales, on the landing hero, **outside every safeguard built for that claim family**
  - **Outside the parity guard:** `tests/test_disclaimer_source_parity.py` covers only the three share/explore files (`utils/i18n-share.ts` · `api/i18n/explore_strings.py` · `api/services/share_renderer.py`). The landing tagline is a **fourth surface** with independent translations — the bn rendering (`আধিকারিক`) is already a **third distinct Bengali word for "official"** across surfaces. Parity is not the fix here (the tagline is a deliberately different, three-sentence sibling of `headerTagline`), but **claim-strength review is**: this is the exact class where MT escalated "official" to **government** (`সরকারি`) on the explore surface, caught at fly 225 only by per-locale review.
  - **Outside the sent 70-row reviewer CSV** (fly 226): that file covers `modalConsentCheckbox` · `settingsRevokeConfirm` · `publicDisclaimer` · `publicShortDisclaimer` · `headerTagline` — not this key.
  - **Spot-checked 2026-08-11, stated at its confidence:** bn does **not** use `সরকারি` (no escalation found); ar uses `متحقق` (a different passive form than the reviewed `تم التحقق`); naturalness and claim-strength in all 14 locales **UNDETERMINED** without native review.
  - **Mitigation (this commit's baton, D3):** `out/vela_legal_i18n_review_addendum_20260811.csv` — 14 rows, 17 columns matching the sent CSV's format, the required name-the-word-for-"official" notes prompt on every row, round-trip PASS. **Generated and AWAITING FOUNDER SEND.** q-columns left empty (the main CSV already collects one answer per language).
  - **Closes when:** the addendum review returns and any corrections are applied. If a correction lands, remember the tagline lives ONLY in `utils/i18n.ts` — one file, no cross-file propagation needed.
  - **🔴 UPDATE 2026-08-11 (coordinator QC, pre-send):** the baseline **`আধিকারিক` is flagged as governmental-leaning** — the same claim-strength class fly 225 fixed on the explore surface (`সরকারি` = government). **The B1 recon's escalation check passed this word because it grepped only for `সরকার` — the PREVIOUS incident's word.** Lesson, verbatim: **"a last-incident-word grep is not a claim-strength check"** — same family as the Rule 20/23 single-sample lessons: the instrument searched for the last failure's signature, not for the failure class.
  - **✅ INTERIM FIX shipped (B2.1 commit 1, Branch A):** the share/explore surfaces use **`অফিসিয়াল`** (the neutral loanword — `utils/i18n-share.ts:611`, `api/i18n/explore_strings.py:370`, converged at fly 225; `verifySub` agrees), NOT আধিকারিক-family — so the landing tagline's "official" word was aligned to `অফিসিয়াল`, one term across all surfaces, rest of the sentence untouched. No parity-guarded file touched (the guard covers the three share/explore files; `utils/i18n.ts` is the fourth surface). Coordinator's neutral candidates (`স্বীকৃত` / `মান্য`) are in the reviewer note for the formal decision.

- [HONESTY] **[P1 · retrieval-corpus integrity / honesty — PROPOSED RATING, founder to ratify; surfaced by the fly-214 citation gate 2026-07-29] The 690-doc LOCAL drug corpus is 100% EMPTY STUBS — every document is field labels with no values (max 5 chars of content) — yet it is Tier-2-boosted (×1.5) into `top_k` and its cards claim "official FDA drug labeling data". The real text (≈3.58M chars) sits UNREAD in the builder's own input**
  - **Measured, corpus-wide (`data/drug_vectordb/index.json`, all 690 docs).** PAYLOAD = content minus the builder's literal template scaffolding (`scripts/build_drug_vectordb.py:63-157`):
    - **190 docs (27.5%) have payload == 0** — nothing but field labels. **All 690 (100%) are ≤ 20 chars. Max = 5. Median = 1.** **190/190 drugs** have no document above 50 chars.
    - By doc_type: `basic_info` 190 docs, **100% payload-0**; `safety` 188, `adverse_reactions` 167, `interactions` 145 — all 100% ≤ 50 chars; **`pharmacology` produced 0 documents** (its `if data.get(...)` guard is never satisfied, so a whole section type silently does not exist).
    - **0 / 690** can ever render a "Show more" control (`CitationPanel` `isLong` needs `abstract.length > 200`). Every DailyMed card can. The founder's screenshot observation was reading a real structural difference.
    - The founder's two cards reproduce exactly from the data: `fda-metformin-adverse` content = `Drug: Metformin\n\nAdverse Reactions:\n6` → renders `Drug: Metformin Adverse Reactions: 6`.
  - **ROOT CAUSE (code-proven, NOT "the data doesn't exist"):** `scripts/collect_drug_data.py` calls `FDAClient.get_drug_label()` → `FDADrugLabel.to_dict()` (`api/data_sources/fda.py:77-91`), **a flat dict of plain strings** — but every `_extract_*` was written for the **raw openFDA JSON** shape, where sections are *lists*: `label_data.get("adverse_reactions", [""])[0]` (`:132`). On a **string**, `[0]` returns **the first character** → `'6'`, `'4'`, `'7'`, `'A'`, `'C'`, `'D'`, `'W'` (**verified 190/190 exact match**, `flat == full_label[field][0]`, zero mismatches). Keys that exist only in the raw shape (`indications_and_usage`, `dosage_and_administration`, `clinical_pharmacology`, `openfda`, `pregnancy`) are absent → `[""]` default → `""`, which is why `indications`/`dosage`/`generic_name`/`brand_names`/`pharmacology` are empty 190/190.
  - **⚠️ THE DATA IS THERE AND UNUSED:** every record keeps `full_label` (`collect_drug_data.py:72`), which the builder never reads — `adverse_reactions` **1.36M chars** (median 5,354), `dosage` 831K, `drug_interactions` 633K, `warnings` 350K, `indications` 286K, `contraindications` 110K = **≈3.58 million characters** of real FDA label text discarded at build.
  - **What they are in retrieval terms:** embeddings are **not** degenerate — mean pairwise cosine 0.467 (within-doc_type 0.588–0.664), **0** twins above 0.95, 690/690 distinct strings. They are cleanly separated **by drug name and section label and by nothing else** — a high-quality embedding of `"<drug> + <section heading>"` with no content behind it. **That is why they reach `top_k` and then contribute nothing.**
  - **CONSEQUENCES:** **(a)** they occupy `top_k` slots and are **weighted UP** (§2.10.6 `local` = Tier 2 ×1.5 → composite ×2.25, above Tier-4 PubMed at ×1.0) — cross-ref the **[P2] Research pool budget** surface (11 safety sections measured at composite rank 5, one slot outside `top_k=5`) and M3's finding that displacement evicts on-point studies, not filler. **(b)** a citation with **no readable content AND no link** is the worst combination for the citation-mandatory positioning. **(c)** ⚠️ **it refutes the premise of a ratified founder decision** — the 2026-07-06 ratification of `local = Tier 2 ×1.5` is reasoned *"local IS cached FDA labeling that relabels to 'FDA'"*; it is not, it is field-label boilerplate. (Consistent with that sweep's own result that local-tier moved **ordering** but **never** citation membership.) See the [P2 · founder-decision] entry below.
  - **The deprecation question is now THREE-way** (was two): **DEPRECATE** (removes the Finding-5 UX problem too) · **REPAIR** (point the builder at `full_label`, ~10 lines + re-embed — but 🔴 changes retrieval input on every Research query, needs its own §2.7 + danger-path re-gate, adds a *second* whole-drug FDA corpus beside the live per-section DailyMed one, and still leaves the URL unlinkable) · **KEEP AS-IS**. **The deciding measurement is cheap and already tooled** — §2.7 20-case Research gate with `local` excluded (floor 18/2/0, per-FAIL adjudication) + pool-identity capture (`tests/run_golden_tests.py`) to classify what fills the freed slots. **One run decides three open items.**
  - **Why [P1] proposed:** user-visible on every Research answer carrying an FDA chip, it degrades retrieval by consuming boosted slots, AND it invalidates the stated basis of a ratified decision. **Founder may down-rate to [P2]** on the grounds that nothing *false* is asserted in the answer text — the same reasoning that put the openFDA entry at [P2]. **Rating is proposed, not assumed.**
  - **✅ MEASURED + RECOMMENDATION READY (2026-07-29, same day) — full record [`docs/local_corpus_decision_20260729.md`](docs/local_corpus_decision_20260729.md). Nothing shipped.**
    - **THE SPLIT (190 records vs the DailyMed corpus):** **REDUNDANT 155 (81.6%)** — DailyMed already has the moiety **with** a whitelisted safety section · **COMPLEMENTARY 35 (18.4%)** · **NEITHER 0**. (Method cross-check: the same script independently reproduces the recorded **128/1038 (12.3%)** no-safety figure.)
    - **The complementary 18.4% is smaller than it looks:** 19 structured-Rx · 14 **OTC Drug-Facts "Warnings" only** · 2 thin; **2 of the 19 are combination-product labels** → **clean structured core = 17 drugs (8.9%)**, 339,272 chars.
    - **🔎 The DailyMed gap is a reference-label SELECTION artifact, not missing data.** **120/128 (93.8%)** of the no-safety moieties carry exactly `{34067-9, 34068-7}` — the **OTC Drug-Facts shape** — and the selected reference label is an **NDA-approved OTC brand** (Aleve · ADVIL · VAZALORE · Prilosec OTC · PEPCID AC · Tagamet · Lamisil AT · Nasacort). The build's `NDA > NDA_AG > ANDA > other` rule has **no Rx-vs-OTC tiebreak**, so it *prefers* OTC brand NDAs over Rx generic ANDAs. For the same moieties openFDA's hit is an **Rx label with real interactions text** (Naproxen 10,898 · Terbinafine 3,570 · Famotidine 1,428 chars). ⚠️ Inference from the openFDA mirror — **not confirmed by a live DailyMed query.** The 23 `absent` drugs (biologics / insulins / GLP-1s / controlled) are the corpus's **declared TFDA-mono-moiety scope boundary**, not a defect.
    - **A repair would import three known defects:** **22/190 (11.6%) full_label records are COMBINATION products** (Metformin → SITAGLIPTIN AND METFORMIN; Lisinopril → LISINOPRIL AND HCTZ; Codeine → ACETAMINOPHEN AND CODEINE) — the same wrong-object family as the [P2] wrong-drug intrusion; **repackager-first selection** (`search_drug_labels_sync(drug, limit=1)`, no reference preference — A-S Medication Solutions ×4, Bryant Ranch ×3, CVS ×2); and **still no URL** (`full_label.url` = `https://labels.fda.gov/` on 190/190).
    - **LIVE §2.7 with `local` EXCLUDED — cost is nil.** Same-day CONTROL (local ON) **18/2/0** vs TREATMENT (local OFF) **19/1/0**; **0 FAIL in both**; both `run_complete=true` / `gate_valid=true` / 20-of-20 / `never_ran=[]` / **exit 0**. **R15 PASS in both, pool IDENTICAL** (adjudicated on its own merits, not pre-accepted). **R17 moved WARN→PASS on a byte-IDENTICAL pool** = judge oscillation — which is exactly why a same-day control was run; against the day-old baseline it would have been miscounted as a regression caused by the removal.
    - **Footprint is 2 documents out of 87** (2.3%) — and **the same two** in both local-ON runs (07-28 baseline and today's control): R01 `fda-metformin-adverse` and R13 `fda-warfarin-interactions`, both 1-character stubs. ⚠️ **On R13 (`warfarin 和 aspirin 一起用 safe 嗎`, a danger-path interaction query) the pool carried a citation titled "Warfarin - Interactions" whose entire content is one character.** Freed slots: R01 → `PMID:38837240` (*Metformin for preventing the progression of CKD*); R13 → **nothing entered, pool 5→3**. Safety sections rose 12→15 but **NOT attributably** — `local` was in neither R10's nor R11's control pool; that is rewrite-nondeterminism churn (11/20 pools changed identity). **Intrusion count 0→0, reported and left to stand — the §2.7 set contains none of the queries the wrong-drug defect was measured on, so this measurement has NO power over it.**
    - **RECOMMENDATION = (c), not (a) alone and explicitly NOT (b):** **(c1) DEPRECATE `local` from Research retrieval**, and **(c2) recover the real gap in the DailyMed build** — (c2-i) an Rx-preference tiebreak in reference-label selection (targets the NAPROXEN/FAMOTIDINE/TERBINAFINE class; **confirm against DailyMed first**), (c2-ii) scope widening for biologics/insulin/GLP-1 (**founder scope call**). Deprecation also closes the Finding-5 UX item, the FDA-chip `local`-vs-openFDA ambiguity, and one side of the sourceLabels↔share_renderer drift for free. **8 gates listed in the decision doc §4** — §2.7 is pre-measured, the danger-path re-gate / canary / prod human-eye gate / `citation_url_guard.mjs` local-exemption update / `/api/status` `get_vector_store()` handling are NOT done.
  - **✅ c1 DEPRECATION BUILT + GATED 2026-07-29 — NOT DEPLOYED.** Build record: [`docs/local_corpus_deprecation_c1_build.md`](docs/local_corpus_deprecation_c1_build.md). `enable_local=False` at `api/server.py:449` (the only retrieval wiring point) **and the `HybridRetriever.__init__` default flipped True→False**; `/api/status` no longer loads the store; builder marked ⛔ do-not-run. **GATES:** §2.7 on the final shipped code **20 PASS / 0 WARN / 0 FAIL** (`run_complete`/`gate_valid` true, 20/20, `never_ran=[]`, exit 0, **0 local docs in 86 pool docs**; R15 PASS adjudicated on merits) · section-aware **danger-path 0 hard violations / 0 rechecks** · **canary** unchanged (no local, no safety intrusion in either arm; GLP-1 run-0 pool identical) · guard **negative-controlled** (re-enabling local FAILS both halves; reverted and re-verified) · **124 pytest passed** · `tsc --noEmit` exit 0. ⚠️ **Do NOT read 20/20 as an improvement** — R17/R20 are known oscillators and R17 moved WARN→PASS earlier the same day on a byte-identical pool; the defensible claim is **no cost**. **Still required before ship: prod human-eye gate** (checklist written in the build record).
  - **⚠️ FOOTPRINT — §2.7 UNDERSTATES local by >10× on the class where it lives** (swept 191 persisted artifacts): §2.7 **2.3%** of pool docs · pool-size harvest **8.3%** · **single-drug OTC safety 30.3%, present in 5 of 6 queries on 3/3 runs**. Only **22 of 690 documents (3.2%)** have ever appeared in any measurement. **AND IT WAS CITED:** on **R13** (`warfarin 和 aspirin 一起用 safe 嗎`) the answer cited `[5] fda-warfarin-interactions` — whose entire content is `Drug: Warfarin\n\nDrug Interactions:\n7`. **A one-character document presented to the user as a source for a drug-interaction safety claim** (1 of the 4 times a local doc reached top_k).
  - **Freed-slot result (paired arms, one process, N=3):** every OTC safety query **retained ≥1 on-target DailyMed safety section in every local-OFF run**; what left was the stub. **Two effects reported, not glossed:** (i) **pools shrink on this class** — aspirin 2.7→**1.0 docs/run** (a single document in all 3 runs), ibuprofen 4.7→2.0, naproxen 5.0→4.0, pushing these queries toward the `pool_size<=2` shape (the content lost is nil, but the founder should know these answers stand on 1–2 documents — an argument **for** c2); (ii) `ibuprofen_warn` safety sections 9→4 is **NOT attributable** — the ON arm's own runs ranged **4, 4, 1**, so within-arm variance already spans the between-arm difference (K-union rewrite nondeterminism); N=3 cannot resolve it and no claim is made.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-07-29 (fly-214 prod human-eye gate). **Measured + recommendation:** 2026-07-29. **c1 built + gated (not deployed):** 2026-07-29. **Evidence:** [`docs/citation_gate_findings_20260729.md`](docs/citation_gate_findings_20260729.md) Finding 1 · [`docs/local_corpus_decision_20260729.md`](docs/local_corpus_decision_20260729.md) · [`docs/local_corpus_deprecation_c1_build.md`](docs/local_corpus_deprecation_c1_build.md). **Nothing deleted — the corpus file and builder are left in place pending founder sign-off.**

- [OTHER] **[P2 · observability wiring — surfaced 2026-08-14 (B4.5 baton item D4), NOT fixed] Sentry's Next.js side is still on the pre-Next-15 layout: no `instrumentation.ts`, so the SDK never initializes server-side, and the browser is still on the deprecated `sentry.client.config.ts` entry point**
  - **`CONFIRMED` from the filesystem, not merely from the log line:** `sentry.client.config.ts`, `sentry.server.config.ts` and `sentry.edge.config.ts` all exist (216 / 216 / 179 bytes, each a bare `Sentry.init`), and **there is no `instrumentation.ts` and no `instrumentation-client.ts`** — checked at repo root and `src/`. The build-time half IS wired: `next.config.ts:2,38` applies `withSentryConfig`. The dependency is `@sentry/nextjs ^10.46.0` (`package.json:15`) — the generation that requires the instrumentation-file layout and deprecates the config-file one. Dev logs match exactly: *"Could not find a Next.js instrumentation file… the Sentry SDK is not initialized on the server"*, plus a `sentry.client.config.ts` deprecation under Turbopack.
  - **⚠️ THE SEVERITY IS NOT WHAT THE WARNING SAYS — the server half is MOOT and the CLIENT half is the real exposure.** `next.config.ts:5` sets `output: 'export'`, so the frontend is a **static export: there is no Next.js server in production** for the SDK to fail to initialize on. Real server-side error reporting is the **FastAPI backend**, which runs its own independent `sentry_sdk.init()` at `api/server.py:17-31` gated on `SENTRY_DSN` — different SDK, different env var (`SENTRY_DSN`, not the frontend's `NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN`), untouched by any of this. **Browser errors are the only Next-side errors that exist for a static export, and those are precisely the ones riding the deprecated entry point.** Do not file this as "server error reporting is dead"; that reading is wrong in both directions.
  - **Why it is filed rather than shrugged off:** a deprecated path that still works is the worst state to be in, because it *looks* present. When `sentry.client.config.ts` stops being loaded — a minor `@sentry/nextjs` bump is enough — **client error reporting goes silently dead with no failing build and no alert**, and the only signal is a dashboard that quietly stops receiving events. Compounding it, all three configs set `enabled: process.env.NODE_ENV === "production"`, so **no amount of dev or preview traffic will ever demonstrate the break**.
  - **Fix shape (NOT applied here):** add `instrumentation.ts` exporting `register()` that imports the server/edge config per `NEXT_RUNTIME`, and rename `sentry.client.config.ts` → `instrumentation-client.ts`. **Verification must be an actual thrown browser error arriving in the Sentry project** — a green build proves nothing about this, which is the whole reason the current state went unnoticed.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-08-14. Deliberately not fixed in B4.5 (landing/UX baton); filed with evidence per the baton.

- [OTHER] **[sec][P2 · webhook idempotency — surfaced 2026-08-17 during Deletion-feature C recon, NOT fixed] Both existing payment webhooks are read-then-write (TOCTOU): a concurrent duplicate delivery returns 500 instead of `already_processed`**
  - **`CONFIRMED` from code.** `/api/webhooks/lemonsqueezy` (`api/server.py:1946`) does `SELECT ... WHERE event_id == event_id` then, later, `db.add(WebhookEvent(...)); db.commit()` (`:1977-1981`, `:2017-2018`). `/api/webhook/dodo` (`:2026`) does the same (`:2083-2085`, `:2173-2174`). Between the SELECT and the INSERT there is a window; `webhook_events.event_id` is the PRIMARY KEY, so a duplicate that arrives inside that window raises `IntegrityError` — and **neither handler catches it**, so the provider sees a **500**. Providers retry on 5xx, so the failure mode is self-amplifying.
  - **Impact is bounded but real:** the row is not double-written (the PK holds), so there is no data corruption. The cost is a spurious 500 + a provider retry storm, and — for Dodo — a misleading `subscription.*` failure signal on a path the deletion SOP's Step 1 depends on for cancellation confirmation.
  - **The fix is known and already demonstrated in-repo:** insert-first, `except IntegrityError: db.rollback(); return already_processed`. `/api/webhooks/clerk` (`:2181`, added 2026-08-17) does exactly this and deliberately did NOT copy the defect — carrying a known defect to a third surface is the same failure mode as failing to carry a mitigation (Rule 19 in reverse).
  - ⚠️ **`_safe_db_write()` (Rule 7) cannot express this fix** — it catches every exception and returns `False`, which would report a database outage as `already_processed`. Any repair here needs the same narrow catch, which is a second de-facto Rule 7 deviation on the webhook surface; **note that neither existing webhook uses `_safe_db_write()` today either.** Worth one ruling covering all three rather than three ad-hoc calls.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-08-17. Not fixed — the baton scoped the Clerk surface only, and repairing live payment paths is not a side-effect-free change.
  - **📌 2026-08-25 ANNOTATIONS (entry stays OPEN, [P2] unchanged).** ① The line refs above have rotted (~+7 at the recon, further moved by the idempotency-key fix) — cite symbols: at current HEAD the LS SELECT is `:1983-1987` with INSERT+commit `:2023-2024`; the Dodo SELECT is `:2095` with INSERT `:2185`. Mechanism unchanged. ② **REACHABILITY CHANGED by `0a7320d`:** pre-fix, real Dodo payloads got a fresh locally-generated key per delivery, so two concurrent duplicates could never share a PK — this race was unreachable-in-practice on the Dodo surface (the price being that idempotency never fired AT ALL, the worse defect, now fixed). Post-fix, concurrent redeliveries share `dodo_{webhook-id}` and the `IntegrityError` → 500 window is real. Sequential replay is now pytest-covered (`test_payment_webhooks.py`); the CONCURRENT window is not — it needs real concurrency the sqlite fixture cannot express. ③ Recon quantified the window: LS is in-process milliseconds (no awaits between SELECT and commit); Dodo spans an awaited Clerk lookup with a 10.0s timeout on the production path. ④ **2026-08-26: the fix is now DEPLOYED (fly 240), so the reachable-concurrent-window state described in ② is LIVE ON PROD** — no longer a local-only property. Entry stays OPEN, [P2] unchanged.

- [OTHER] **[P2 · correctness gap, NO OBSERVED USER HARM — measured 2026-08-15, real-device tested 2026-08-17] `/media/*.mp4` serves no HTTP byte ranges: `Range` is ignored, the whole file is returned with `200`**
  - **Measured on prod (fly 233):** `Range: bytes=0-1023` → **`200`** with the full **2,325,186 bytes**, **no `Accept-Ranges`, no `Content-Range`**. All four range forms behave identically (`bytes=0-1023`, `bytes=100-`, `bytes=-500`, and the unsatisfiable `bytes=999999999-`, which a correct server would answer `416`).
  - **Cause, established by reading the installed code, not inferred:** `fastapi==0.111.0` requires `starlette<0.38.0,>=0.37.2`, and the installed **starlette 0.37.2** has **zero** range handling — `staticfiles.py` contains no `range`/`206`/`Content-Range`/`Accept-Ranges` markers at all, and `FileResponse.__call__` streams the whole file unconditionally after setting only `content-length`/`last-modified`/`etag`. **Range support landed in Starlette 0.39.0 — NOT 0.45**, established by downloading the wheels for 0.38.0/0.38.6/0.39.0/0.40–0.45 and reading `responses.py` out of each: 0.38.6 has none; 0.39.0 has `accept-ranges`, `"status": 206` and `_parse_range_header`.
  - 🔴 **THE ORIGINAL PREDICTION WAS WRONG AND IS RECORDED AS WRONG.** The first write-up said *"iOS Safari requires range support to play `<video>`, so the demo will very likely NOT play on iPhone."* **Founder real-device test 2026-08-17: the video PLAYS on iPhone and SEEK WORKS.** Both predicted harms did not occur. A browser that simply fetches the whole file is **consistent** with seeking working. **Both truths stand together: the mechanism is real and measured, and no user-visible harm was observed.**
  - ⚠️ **Evidence boundary — ONE 2.3 MB file, ONE device, ONE network.** This does **not** clear the gap for larger media or poor mobile networks, where fetching the entire file before playback is precisely the failure ranges exist to prevent. Rated `[P2]` on that basis, not on any observed breakage.
  - **Fix path:** bump to **`fastapi==0.115.2`** (the first release whose constraint `starlette<0.41.0,>=0.37.2` admits a range-capable Starlette; 0.115.1 still caps at `<0.39.0`) **AND add an EXPLICIT `starlette==` pin.** 🔴 **The explicit pin is the load-bearing half:** the widened range still *contains* the rangeless 0.37/0.38 line, so an unpinned rebuild could silently resolve backwards and restore the bug — and **nothing in `/health` would reveal it**, since `/health` reports only `status`, `version` and the git `revision`. `pydantic==2.8.2` is unaffected (the constraint is identical across 0.111 → 0.116).
  - ✅ **2026-09-01 (fly 241) — PAGE-LOAD COST MEASURED ON PROD AND EXCLUDED BY EVIDENCE, entry stays OPEN.** The landing now ships `preload="metadata"` with no poster (Gate 8 row 5 ruling), which was this entry's worst-case trigger — and the founder measured the actual page-load transfer of `/media/research-demo.mp4`: **9.3 kB, Status 200, 133 ms** — the browser aborts the rangeless 200 stream after reading metadata, so the feared whole-file 2.3 MB fetch **did NOT materialize at page load**. **Residual scope, which is why this does NOT close:** seek behaviour and larger files — a browser that must seek into a rangeless stream still has no `206` path, and the fly-233 evidence boundary (one 2.3 MB file, one device, one network) still applies there. Fix path above unchanged.
  - ⚠️ **Verification cost is the real price, not the bump.** The bump itself is one line. But **SSE streaming (`/api/research`, `/api/explain`) and BOTH payment webhooks have ZERO automated coverage** — only 4 of 24 test files import the app at all, and the only webhook-named test (`tests/test_webhook_cancel.py`) `sys.exit(1)`s at import and is excluded from every run. The 291-passing suite runs *alongside* those surfaces, it does not cover them. Any bump needs a manual SSE stream and a replayed webhook signature.
  - 🔴 **RULE 20 — THE ARTIFACT IS NOT RETAINED, AND THAT IS SAID RATHER THAN IMPLIED.** Every number above came from throwaway probe scripts in a session scratchpad (a prod `fetch` matrix and a wheel-diff script). **There is no committed script and no result JSON.** They were one-off diagnostics against a live external host and a package index, so re-running them would produce a *new* snapshot rather than the one these findings rest on. **Do not cite these figures as reproducible evidence without first re-measuring.** If this entry is ever acted on, the re-measurement belongs in `tests/probes/` per Rule 20.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-08-15 (fly 233 spot-checks). **Prediction refuted:** 2026-08-17.

- [OTHER] **[P3 · caching — surfaced 2026-08-17, NOT fixed] No `Cache-Control` on any static asset, including the 2.22 MB demo video, so every visit revalidates**
  - **Measured on prod (fly 233):** `HEAD /media/research-demo.mp4` returns `Content-Length`, `Content-Type`, `ETag` and `Last-Modified` — and **no `Cache-Control`**. Starlette's `FileResponse`/`StaticFiles` set none by default and nothing in `api/server.py` adds one.
  - **Effect is bounded, which is why it is `[P3]`:** `ETag`/`Last-Modified` are present and conditional requests DO return `304`, so a repeat visitor re-validates rather than re-downloading. The cost is a round-trip per asset per visit, not 2.22 MB per visit. But the hashed `/_next/static/*` bundles are content-addressed and immutable and could safely be `max-age=31536000, immutable`; they are re-validated too.
  - **Same root as the entry above** — both are consequences of serving the Next.js export through Starlette's defaults with no cache policy layered on. Worth fixing together if either is touched.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-08-17, during the fly-233 range investigation. Same Rule 20 caveat: the header observations came from a scratchpad probe, not a committed artifact.

- [OTHER] **[P2 · retrieval-corpus coverage — measured 2026-08-04, INDEPENDENT of whether c2 ships] The DailyMed corpus silently drops the warnings section of 264 PRESCRIPTION labels because LOINC `34071-1` is not in the builder's `SECTIONS` — 914,222 characters of prescriber-facing safety text**
  - **What:** `scripts/build_dailymed_label_corpus.py:77-84` whitelists `34073-7 · 34066-1 · 34070-3 · 43685-7 · 34067-9 · 34068-7`. **`34071-1` — the PRE-PLR "WARNINGS" code — is absent.** It is **not an OTC-only code**: legacy-format **prescription** labels never converted to the Physician Labeling Rule use it for warnings, while `43685-7` is the PLR-era "WARNINGS AND PRECAUTIONS".
  - **Measured** (`CONFIRMED`; 1036/1038 pinned setids re-parsed, no re-selection, no `--resolve`): **381 labels (36.8%) carry `34071-1`, 985,163 chars total. Of those, 264 are PRESCRIPTION labels carrying 914,222 chars — 93% of the total** (median 2,105, max 20,186). Only 107 are OTC labels (66,480 chars). **118 of the 128 moieties with zero safety sections would gain their first** from this code alone.
  - **Impact:** for those 264 Rx labels the corpus holds interactions/contraindications/boxed but **not warnings**, so a warnings-shaped question can retrieve a *sibling drug's* warnings while the queried drug's own warnings sit unindexed.
  - **🔁 Belongs to the instrument-blind class (7th instance):** the corpus's coverage metric counts *whitelisted* sections, so a label whose only warnings live in `34071-1` scores as "no safety section" — **the measurement inherits the omission it should expose.** That is how "120/128 OTC-shaped" came to imply an OTC problem when **93% of the missing text is Rx**.
  - **⚠️ Fixing it is NOT free and is NOT a c2 substitute:** +381 docs (+8.3%) into a `top_k=5` budget already measured tight, and **measurement shows it does not remove the wrong-drug citation on any of 5 queries** ([`docs/c2_phase1b_measurement_20260804.md`](docs/c2_phase1b_measurement_20260804.md) Part 3). 🆕 **2026-08-05 — WORSE THAN INEFFECTIVE ON ONE QUERY: E-A INTRODUCED a new wrong-object citation.** `UVADEX (Methoxsalen) — Drug Interactions` — a **psoralen**, unrelated to ibuprofen — was cited in **3/3 treatment runs and 0/27 control runs** on `ibuprofen warnings and precautions`. ⚠️ **E-A did not add that document**: UVADEX `#34073-7` is in the **shipped** corpus already and had never been cited; E-A's +381 rows perturbed ranking enough to surface it. ⚠️ **Scope, stated honestly: N=3 per arm, ONE query.** This is **one observed instance**, not a general introduction rate — but it is on the arm that was supposed to help. Evidence: `tests/probes/c2/c2_ab_retrieval.json`, re-derived offline by `setid → moiety`. A narrower **`E-A-Rx`** (Rx doctype only: +264 docs, 0% lay language) is defined in [`docs/c2_phase1c_20260804.md`](docs/c2_phase1c_20260804.md) Part 4 — **also untested.** **Record the coverage defect; sequence any fix with c2, not ahead of it.**
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-08-04.

- [OTHER] **[P2 · data staleness — measured 2026-08-04] The DailyMed corpus has NO refresh mechanism at all — only an intent string — and has already drifted**
  - **`CONFIRMED` from code/data, not inferred:** `_meta.monthly_re_pull` exists but is **prose** (*"re-run with `--resolve`; snapshot pinned by db_published_date below"*); **`db_published_date` is `None`** — the pin field the note points at is not populated; **there is no `.github/workflows/`** (no CI, no cron); the only other reference is `BUILD_NOTES.md` documenting the manual command. **There is no schedule, no automation, and no dated pin. The corpus has been frozen since it was built.**
  - **Already measurably drifted:** re-parsing the **same pinned setids** against the shipped corpus gives **30 sections whose content DIFFERS — 18 of them SAFETY sections** (Warnings ×10, Interactions ×4, Contraindications ×2, Boxed ×2), with substantive deltas: RILUZOLE warnings **+741**, PROGESTERONE contraindications **+305** (we are missing text the live label has), BUPRENORPHINE warnings **−661** (we serve text no longer in the label).
  - **❌ CORRECTED 2026-08-04 — the "11 `source_id`s no longer exist upstream" claim in the first version of this entry is REFUTED.** All 11 belonged to the **2 setids the probe itself failed to fetch** (TOLTERODINE TARTRATE, ZOLPIDEM TARTRATE). **Genuinely vanished upstream: 0.** Those labels are not withdrawn — `drugInfo.cfm` serves **Detrol® LA** and **AMBIEN CR** at HTTP 200; only the v2 API XML 404s. **A DailyMed API-vs-web inconsistency that blocks the REFRESH path, not citations.** ⚠️ **Design note for any future refresh: it must FAIL LOUD on a 404 rather than treat missing sections as deletions** — that misreading is exactly what produced this false claim.
  - **🔴 The user-visible consequence of the 30 (measured):** the setid still resolves, so **the deep-link opens the CURRENT label while our index holds SUPERSEDED text**. The chip renders, the link works, the label is real — **and the quoted text is a stale revision, with nothing in the UI signalling a version.** Same honesty family as the pre-c1 empty stubs on `/explore`, but harder to spot: **an empty stub is visibly empty; drifted text is plausible and was true when captured.** Detail: [`docs/dailymed_stale_citations_20260804.md`](docs/dailymed_stale_citations_20260804.md).
  - **Same family as the overdue TFDA id=37 re-pull** (STATE open-item #6) but on the **DailyMed** side — and worse, because TFDA at least has a dated snapshot + a manifest note, while this has a null pin date. **They should share one "external data goes stale on a schedule" mechanism.**
  - **💰 COST MODEL CORRECTED 2026-08-04 — the re-embed is NOT the expensive part.** This entry previously implied a full re-embed was the cost gate. **A FULL re-embed of the entire 4,608-doc corpus is $0.063** (12.65 M chars ≈ 3.16 M tokens @ $0.02/1M). **The real costs are network time (~25 min for `--resolve`), 🔴 gate cycles, and blast radius** — and a *selective* re-embed of only the changed rows is available (v197 precedent; row-index alignment lets unchanged vectors be preserved).
  - **✅ M3 IS FEASIBLE AND CHEAP — measured 2026-08-04:** **all 13 drifted labels bumped `spl_version`; zero silent revisions.** So **drift is detectable by version comparison alone — no XML parse, no diff, no embed** (~1038 cheap JSON queries). **M3 changes nothing about retrieval, so it is NOT 🔴 and needs no gate.** Filed as its own BACKLOG item, **independent of c2**.
  - **🔴 EVERY refresh path is 🔴, including a plain re-pull with no selection change** — because the **section text itself changes** (30 sections, 18 safety, in both directions). **A refresh is not a maintenance no-op.**
  - **⚠️ And no refresh path fixes the wrong-drug citation** — supplying a correct document does not displace an incorrect one ([`docs/c2_phase1c_20260804.md`](docs/c2_phase1c_20260804.md) §0). **Do not sequence a refresh as a wrong-drug fix.**
  - **No cadence proposed — founder call.** ⚠️ Note the ordering: **the cadence cannot be chosen rationally until M3 produces a drift *rate*.** Today there is one snapshot against a **null pin date**, so the interval is unknown.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-08-04. **Evidence:** [`docs/c2_phase1c_20260804.md`](docs/c2_phase1c_20260804.md) Part 5.3 + [`docs/c2_phase1b_measurement_20260804.md`](docs/c2_phase1b_measurement_20260804.md) Part 2.

- [HONESTY] **[P2 · wrong-object / honesty — measured 2026-08-04] 15 pinned DailyMed reference labels are NOT human drug labels; at least 7 are VETERINARY, and 2 of those are CITABLE as safety sources**
  - **`CONFIRMED`, all 15 named** in [`docs/c2_phase1c_20260804.md`](docs/c2_phase1c_20260804.md) Part 2. **All 15 contribute ≥1 indexed section, so all are retrievable in principle. 3 carry a WHITELISTED SAFETY section**, i.e. can be cited as a safety source:
    - **`CHLORTETRACYCLINE HCL` → "CLTC 100 MR"** — medicated feed, carries **43685-7 warnings** 🔴
    - **`DISODIUM CLODRONATE` → "OSPHOS"** — equine, carries **34070-3 contraindications** 🔴
    - `GLOBULIN` → **Gamunex-C** (Immune Globulin Human) — a legitimate human biologic; only its document type is unusual, **not part of the concern**
  - **Veterinary by the labels' own titles and dosage forms** (evidence, not a guess about the LOINC codes): a **"Soluble Drench Powder"** (LEVAMISOLE), **OvuGel** (swine), **OSPHOS** (equine), **Incurin** (canine), **RenaKare** (feline/canine), **Tetroxy HCA**, **CLTC 100 MR**. The remaining 5 are supplement/medical-food shapes (D-Vite, Ferrous Gluconate, Super Fiber, Florasync, IFEREX 150).
  - **Why this is a MORE serious member of the wrong-object family than combination products:** a combination label is at least a **human** drug containing the queried moiety; **a veterinary label is not a human drug at all.** A Research answer about chlortetracycline or clodronate in a human patient can cite a veterinary label's safety section.
  - ⚠️ **The LOINC document types (`50575-0`, `50577-6`, `50578-4`, `58476-3`, `60683-0`) are deliberately NOT named** — that is regulatory classification and is not being best-guessed. The recordable facts are the codes, the titles, and the citability. **No fix proposed.**
  - **✅ SCOPE CONFIRMED BOUNDED AT 15 (2026-08-04).** A sweep of **all 1036** pinned references by **title, dosage form and species evidence** found **0 additional** non-human products — the doctype signal is *sufficient* here, not a partial catch. ⚠️ A first pass flagged 69 and claimed 2 outside the 15; **both were false positives** and the rejected markers are recorded: `bolus` (standard **human** IV dosing, 40+ oncology/anaesthesia labels), `premix` (**LEVAQUIN/moxifloxacin = a premixed IV bag**, not medicated feed), `drinking water`.
  - **🔴 SEVERITY UPDATE — the two citable sections are NOT equally bad:**
    - **`OSPHOS` contraindications names the species in the sentence** — *"Do not use in **horses** with impaired renal function"* — **a reader can see it is wrong.**
    - **`CLTC 100 MR` "Warnings and Precautions" is WORSE.** It is **occupational-hygiene guidance for feed-mill workers** — *"For use in manufacturing medicated animal feeds only … **Human exposure should therefore be minimized**"* — **not drug safety information at all**, and under a citation chip its wording **reads as a clinical caution**. **Record this one as the more serious of the two.**
  - **✅ RISK IS NOT REALISED — an OBSERVED ZERO, not an absence of observation (2026-08-04):** a read-only query against the **confirmed prod database** found **0 veterinary/supplement setids in any stored `/q/` or `/explore` citation**. **This changes urgency, not whether the fix is right.** ⚠️ Covers **persisted** artifacts only — a transient answer never shared leaves no record.
  - **💰 EXCLUSION COST — cheaper than expected:** documents and embeddings align **by row index** (`vector_store.py:79-99`), so index-time or retrieval-time exclusion is a **parallel row deletion — 27 of 4608 docs (0.59%), $0, NO re-embed.** ⚠️ **But all 15 moieties have exactly ONE reference setid, so exclusion leaves them with ZERO documents.**
  - **❓ OPEN QUESTION (unmeasured):** for these moieties, **does a human single-moiety label exist on DailyMed at all?** **`CHOLECALCIFEROL` is the worked example** — a supplement label today, a **13-ingredient INFUVITE** under E-B, **zero** under exclusion: **no option yields a human mono vitamin-D label.** **If none exists, zero may be the honest answer rather than a coverage hole.** ⚠️ **The implementation is cheap; the gate is not — any exclusion is still 🔴** (own §2.7 + danger-path re-gate + canary + prod human-eye gate).
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-08-04. **Scope + severity + cost:** [`docs/nonhuman_label_scope_20260804.md`](docs/nonhuman_label_scope_20260804.md); prod-zero + E-B interaction: [`docs/dailymed_refresh_cost_20260804.md`](docs/dailymed_refresh_cost_20260804.md) Parts 1 + 5.

- [HONESTY] **[P1 · honesty / wrong-drug citation — TWO LIVE PROD INSTANCES captured at the fly-215 gate 2026-08-03] `aspirin contraindications` cites ACECLOFENAC as its SOLE source, and Reye's syndrome is STRUCTURALLY UNREACHABLE**
  - **Not measurements — user-visible production output**, in founder screenshots at the fly-215 human-eye gate.
    - **🔴 A1 `aspirin contraindications and who should not take it` → `Clanza (Aceclofenac) — Contraindications`, the ONLY citation.** The answer states aspirin is contraindicated in patients allergic to *"other NSAIDs, or related drugs such as **diclofenac**"* — **"diclofenac" appears only because the aceclofenac label reads "other analogues (diclofenac)"**. Reproduced locally on the shipped config: pool of 1, every claim cited to the aceclofenac section, `diclofenac`/`asthma`/`peptic ulcer` all present and all traceable to that label.
    - **🟠 A2 `ibuprofen warnings and precautions` → `[1] Piroxicam — Boxed Warning`.** Same shape, milder: the answer hedges to NSAID class-level language (matching the Phase-1 🟠 adjudication).
  - **⚠️ THE CLINICAL COST — Reye's syndrome is not merely absent, it is UNRETRIEVABLE:** **1 of 4,608** DailyMed documents mentions Reye, and it is **LEVOCARNITINE's Indications** section; the DailyMed `ASPIRIN` moiety has **only Dosage + Indications, no safety section**. Meanwhile aspirin's openFDA `full_label.warnings` is 2,292 chars and **OPENS with** *"Warnings **Reye's syndrome**: Children and teenagers who have or are recovering from chicken pox or flu-like symptoms should not use this product."* **So wrong-drug citation does not only misattribute — it OMITS what the correct label would have supplied**, here aspirin's most distinctive (and paediatric) contraindication.
  - **⚖️ Stated fairly:** the answer does **not** claim completeness — its *Missing Information* section names *"children with viral infections"* as an unaddressed contraindication. **The generator hedged honestly about the gap it could detect.** The **citation** is 🔴; the **answer** stops short of a false clinical claim.
  - **NEITHER IS CAUSED BY c1** — both were measured pre-c1. **c1 made A1 starker**: aspirin's pool thinned 2.7→1.0 docs/run, so the wrong-drug citation went from *one of several* to **the only one**. **The defect did not grow; nothing is left standing in front of it** — c1 uncovering rather than creating, as already recorded.
  - **➡️ STRONGEST EVIDENCE YET FOR c2. Both queries become named rows in c2's human-eye gate.** Cross-ref the [P2] DailyMed coverage entry's 5-for-5 mapping (now **6-for-6** — see the aspirin correction there).
  - **Discovered:** 2026-08-03 (fly-215 prod human-eye gate). **Evidence:** [`docs/local_corpus_deprecation_c1_build.md`](docs/local_corpus_deprecation_c1_build.md) Finding A.

- [HONESTY] **[P2 · honesty / persisted artifacts — surfaced by the c1 Task-3.7.2 re-verification 2026-08-03] Published `/explore` and `/q/` pages serve pre-c1 EMPTY LOCAL STUBS indefinitely — under a credibility pill reading "Official"**
  - ✅ **ADDRESSED AND GATE-PASSED ON PROD — fly 216, 2026-08-07.** Founder-run human-eye gate: **4/4 PASS**, plus both rows that no unit test could check. Completed form: [`docs/render_gate_checklist.md`](docs/render_gate_checklist.md) Gate 1.
    - **Rows 1–4 all matched expectations exactly** — slots 2/5/5/3, tombstones at [2] · [1][4][5] · [1][4][5] · [2], **no credibility pill on any tombstone**, every prose `[N]` still resolving. Source chips counted **only real sources** (`PubMed: 1` / `2` / `2`) — tombstones excluded, as implemented. Non-local citations untouched: green PubMed titles, Peer Reviewed pills, View-source links all intact. **Row 4 (`explore_page`, `server.py:2676`) was confirmed independently of rows 1–3.**
    - 🔴 **LEGIBILITY — PASS, and this is the row that justified building a form at all.** `#a0aec0` reads clearly against the page background; a tombstone card beside a PubMed card is unmistakable at a glance (no coloured source name, no pill, no link, visibly dimmer). Grey-on-grey was the named risk, `#a0aec0` was **inherited rather than chosen**, and **no unit test could have checked it**.
    - **LOCALE — PASS, and stronger than the criterion asked.** Row 2 renders 「來源已撤回」 **and** `參考來源` / `同儕審查` / `檢視來源 ↗` — the locale path resolves **end to end**, not a single-string hit that could have masked a broken path.
    - ✅ **The gate VINDICATED keeping `abstract_truncated` visible:** `[5] Warfarin - Safety` rendered as *"Drug: Warfarin Contraindications: 4 Warnings and Precautions:"* — **truncated mid-field because that field had no value at all.** The reader sees the emptiness directly; hidden, the slot would read as withdrawn for no evident reason.
    - ⚠️ **TECH_DEBT:110 IS NOT CLOSED BY THIS GATE.** This gate used the **RENDER** form (`render_gate_checklist.md`). `:110` closes on a **Research gate that used the OWNERSHIP form** (`human_eye_gate_checklist.md`) — **which still has never been used.** Different form, different question; do not conflate them. *(2026-09-01 update: the founder RAN the ownership form on prod after fly 241 — 8/8 reported PASS — but the per-row OBSERVED OWNER cells were NOT transcribed into the form, so no completed ownership-form record exists and the closure condition remains unmet. Stays open.)*
    - ⚠️ **The deployed commit SHA could NOT be confirmed by readback** — `fly releases` carries no SHA, `fly image show` shows no LABEL, `/health` returns a hardcoded semver. The fly-216 build was tied to `a7e47c2` by **build-provenance inference only**. See the `[P2 · build provenance]` entry below.
  - ✅ **Implementation (2026-08-07) — TOMBSTONED at render time (`api/services/share_renderer.py`), NOT deleted from the DB.** A `source_type == 'local'` citation keeps its slot and its `[N]` but renders with **no credibility pill and no source label** — it reads *"Source withdrawn"* / *"來源已撤回"* — and is excluded from the source-chip summary. Tests: `tests/test_share_local_tombstone.py` (6, and **5 of 6 verified to FAIL against the pre-change code**; the 6th is a documented forward guard). Full suite **161 passed**. **Gate is a human-eye check of the 4 URLs — NOT run yet, and no deploy.**
  - **🔴 THE 8 STUB CITATIONS REMAIN IN THE PROD DB, DELIBERATELY — do not "clean them up".** `api/templates/q_public.jinja2:51` numbers citations by **`loop.index`**, not by a stored field, while the stored `answer_text` cites `[N]` **by position**. Deleting array elements therefore **silently re-points the prose at the WRONG source** — measured on all four rows: e.g. `7xI-q0dxnVg` prose cites [1]–[5]; filtering leaves two entries renumbered to [1],[2] while the prose still means [2],[3]. Plain suppression was **rejected for the same reason**: preserving the index instead leaves **3 of 5 prose markers dangling** on the two viewed rows. `test_prose_marker_alignment_is_preserved` is the regression guard.
  - **Population re-verified read-only 2026-08-06 — unchanged at 4 of 18** (`shared_query` 15 / 12 public / 0 flagged; `explore_page` 3). All 8 stubs carry `credibility:'official'` and `url:''`. **No row is local-only** — each keeps 1/2/2/2 real PubMed citations.
  - ⚠️ **NOT resolved by this change — the upstream write path stays open.** See **[P2 · API contract] `/api/share/create` persists client-supplied citations unvalidated** below: `enable_local=False` stops retrieval from *producing* a local citation, not from one being *stored*, so the population is closed against new retrievals and **open against replays**.
  - **Live right now on a sitemap-listed SEO page.** `/explore/metformin-contraindications-renal?locale=en` renders 5 citation cards, one of which is:
    ```
    [2] Local   ·  Official
    Metformin - Safety
    Drug: Metformin Contraindications: 4 Warnings and Precautions:
    ```
    A **1-character payload** presented as an **Official** source. c1 stopped *new* answers from carrying local stubs; it cannot touch what is already stored, and `share_renderer` renders these **on demand** from stored citation JSON on every request.
  - **The retained `local` label mapping is what keeps these pages rendering at all, so retaining it was correct** — but *"renders correctly"* and *"says something true"* are different bars and only the first is met. This is the same honesty defect c1 removed from the live path, **frozen into indexed URLs**.
  - **Scope is bounded and should be measured before anything is proposed:** only **1** `/explore` slug exists today (per `sitemap-explore.xml`); the `/q/` share population is unknown from outside the DB. **No data migration proposed here** — options range from leaving it (1 page), to suppressing `local` citations at render time for stored artifacts, to a one-off backfill. **Founder call, and it needs the `/q/` count first.**
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-08-03. **Evidence:** [`docs/local_corpus_deprecation_c1_build.md`](docs/local_corpus_deprecation_c1_build.md) §3.7.2 follow-up.

- [OTHER] **[P3 · dead code — surfaced by the c1 blast-radius sweep 2026-07-29, FILED 2026-08-03 (previously recorded only in a session doc)] `scripts/init_knowledge_base.py` is DEAD **and** BROKEN — it would crash on its first call**
  - **What:** it calls `VectorStore(persist_directory=…, collection_name=…)` and `vector_store.add_documents(...)`. The real signature is **`VectorStore(index_path=…)`** and **no `add_documents` method exists** — both are Chroma-era leftovers from before the NumPy JSON index. It is referenced **nowhere**: not in CLAUDE.md's First-Time Setup, not in the Dockerfile, not by any script or test (only its own docstring names it).
  - **Why it matters despite being dead:** CLAUDE.md's setup section teaches new-environment bootstrapping, and a plausible-looking `init_knowledge_base.py` sitting in `scripts/` is a trap for exactly that reader — it will fail with a confusing `TypeError`, not an informative message. Same family as **Rule 8** (`fda_cached.py` is dead code — do not import).
  - **Proposed: DELETE.** Not acted on — deleting a script is founder's call. If kept, it needs a ⛔ do-not-run header like the one added to `build_drug_vectordb.py`.

- [OTHER] **[P3 · public-page / i18n — surfaced while resolving the c1 Task-3.7.2 discrepancy 2026-08-03] A sitemap-listed public SEO page returns a RAW FastAPI JSON 404 to a zh-TW visitor — and `?locale=zh` works while `?locale=zh-TW` does not**
  - **Measured:** `/explore/metformin-contraindications-renal` → `?locale=en` **200** · no param **200** · **`?locale=zh` 200** · **`?locale=zh-TW` 404 `{"detail":"Not found."}`**. Reproduced on both `vela-ai-medical.fly.dev` and `vela.an-tho.com`.
  - **Cause:** `explore_renderer.render_explore_page` (`:147-163`) queries a row keyed by **(slug, locale)** and only an `en` row was authored. The perverse part: **`zh` is NOT a first-class locale so it degrades to `en` and succeeds, while `zh-TW` IS first-class, is therefore honoured, and then fails the row lookup.** The supported locale is the one that breaks.
  - **Why it matters:** the response is **raw JSON**, not a styled 404 or an English fallback — a poor result for a public SEO surface, and Vela's primary market is zh-TW. The sitemap only advertises `?locale=en`, which bounds crawler exposure, but an in-app zh-TW user navigating there sees `{"detail":"Not found."}`.
  - **Fix-direction (not scoped):** fall back to the `en` row when a locale-specific row is absent (matching the `zh` behaviour), or return a styled localized 404. **Not caused by c1; unrelated to the deprecation.**
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-08-03.

- [OTHER] **[P2 · measurement-instrument drift — CAUGHT DURING the c1 ship 2026-07-29, FIXED at the default] NINE measurement harnesses construct `HybridRetriever()` bare, so they matched production BY ACCIDENT — the moment production changed a source flag they would have kept measuring a DEAD SOURCE**
  - **How it was caught:** the **first** section-aware danger-path re-gate for c1 returned a clean 0 violations — and its log said **`✅ Vector store loaded: 690 documents`**. `scripts/dailymed_danger_path_verify.py:181` calls `HybridRetriever()` with no arguments, inheriting the library default `enable_local=True`, so it measured the **pre-ship** configuration. **That result was discarded, not reported.** It was caught by reading the harness's own startup log rather than trusting its verdict.
  - **Nine bare call sites:** `dailymed_danger_path_verify` · `direction_shadow_eval` · `pathb_recall_probe` · `pathb_scope_probe` · `question_neutralization_eval` · `retrieval_recall_check` · `source_weight_activation_verify` · `source_weight_local_tier_sweep` · `source_weight_shadow_eval`.
  - **✅ FIXED AT THE DEFAULT, not the call site** — `HybridRetriever.__init__` `enable_local: bool = False` (`api/rag/retriever.py:96`), so the class is closed rather than one instance. Call sites that genuinely want the corpus pass `enable_local=True` explicitly and are unaffected (**124 tests pass**).
  - **🔁 FIFTH instance of the "instrument blind to its own target" class** — and the **first caught during the change** rather than months later. See the class table on the [P2 · founder-decision] local-tier entry below.
  - **⚠️ THE `enable_local` DEFAULT FIX CLOSES THE PARAMETER, NOT THE CLASS — stated plainly, correcting an over-claim in the c1 build record.** The class is: **nine harnesses each construct their own retriever instead of sharing production's construction path**, so **any** future divergence (a new flag, a new source, a changed weight, a changed call argument) will silently re-open with **no signal**. Flipping one default fixed one instance of the symptom.
  - **✅ FULL DIVERGENCE SWEEP RUN 2026-07-30 (all 9 harnesses × every construction parameter AND every `retrieve()` argument, vs `api/server.py:449`/`:828`). Result — one clean, two real:**
    - **(1) CONSTRUCTION PARAMETERS: ZERO divergence. A real result, so it is stated as one.** After the default flip, production's explicit construction (`local_threshold=0.6 · enable_local=False · enable_pubmed=True · enable_fda=True · enable_tfda=True`, `enable_dailymed` defaulted) is **byte-equivalent to the constructor defaults**, so all nine bare-construction harnesses match production on **all six** parameters. ⚠️ **This equivalence is COINCIDENTAL and load-bearing** — the moment production overrides any flag again, all nine silently diverge.
    - **(2) ⚠️ `retrieve()` ARGUMENTS DIVERGE — `source_weight_active`.** Signature default is **`False`** (`retriever.py:147`); **production passes `True`** (`server.py:827` reads `SOURCE_WEIGHT_ACTIVE`, ON in prod since v200). Harnesses that OMIT it measure the **composite reorder DISABLED** — a ranking path production has not used since **2026-07-06**. **Accidental** (the harness intends production behaviour): `direction_shadow_eval.py:78` · `question_neutralization_eval.py:58` · `tests/results/_neut_probe.py:42`. **Deliberate** (the harness's *purpose* is ON-vs-OFF or shadow-sink measurement, so this is correct): `source_weight_activation_verify.py:66` (OFF arm; `:63` is the ON arm) · `source_weight_shadow_eval.py:57` · `source_weight_local_tier_sweep.py:60`. **Matching production:** `dailymed_danger_path_verify.py:134` passes `source_weight_active=True` ✅.
    - **✅ NO RECORDED GATE RESULT IS INVALIDATED — checked, not assumed.** The only *gate* among the nine is `dailymed_danger_path_verify`, which matches production. And the three accidental cases (`direction_shadow_eval`, `question_neutralization_eval`, `_neut_probe`) were all run **2026-06-17, before v200 activated on 2026-07-06** — at the time they ran they *did* match production. **The exposure is on FUTURE runs, not on the historical record.**
    - **(3) ⚠️ TWO HARNESSES DO NOT CALL `retrieve()` AT ALL — they HAND-MIRROR its stages** through private methods: `pathb_recall_probe.py:66` and `retrieval_recall_check.py:55-57` call `_search_local` / `_search_pubmed` / `_search_fda` directly. **They therefore model a THREE-source pipeline — no TFDA, no DailyMed** — while production fans out to five. This is a deeper divergence than a wrong argument: it re-implements the thing it measures, and drifts every time `retrieve()` changes.
    - **(3b) …and it now fails QUIET, not loud.** With `enable_local=False`, `self.vector_store is None`, so `_search_local` raises `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'search'` — but `retriever.py:593-601` wraps the body in `except Exception` and returns `[]`, logging only `WARNING Local search error: …`. **Verified live.** So those two harnesses now silently measure **zero local documents** behind a warning that reads like a transient search failure. **Production is unaffected** (`:191` guards the call on `enable_local`), so this is harness-only exposure — but the broad `except` converting a programming error into a warning is worth recording on its own.
  - **🔁 THIRD RECURRENCE OF THE MIRROR-NOT-THE-THING DEFECT — appended 2026-08-23, NOT re-rated.** The `(3)` bullet above records harnesses that hand-mirror `retrieve()`'s stages. That shape has now cost three distinct things in the 主線 A line, and they are listed together because the pattern is the finding:
    - **(i) `seat_v2` seat-survival needs RE-MEASURING after any build.** Its own `method_per_stage.mirror_caveat` says so: *"Stages are a MIRROR of `retrieve():208-292`, not `retrieve()` itself… only the dedup/sort/slice arithmetic is mirrored line-for-line. This is the instrument-blindness class of TECH_DEBT #8, unavoidable without instrumenting `api/`, and declared rather than hidden."* Declared honestly — and still a debt.
    - **(ii) THE `13/20 QUERIES END BELOW 5` FIGURE CAME FROM THE MIRROR AND WAS OVERTURNED.** It was reported as a Research-wide property; the real-set figure is **41% pre-c1 / 47% post-c1**. See the premise-integrity entry above.
    - **(iii) AN OFFLINE MIRROR WAS THE OPTION EXPLICITLY REJECTED for measuring the relevance filter — on these grounds** — and the alternative that was used instead needed **no `api/` change at all**, because production already logs the pair at `retriever.py:531`. **The mirror was not the only way to avoid touching `api/`; reading what production already emits was.** That option had not previously been looked for.
  - **📋 PROPOSED (do NOT build here; founder call): a single shared production-construction helper** — e.g. `api/rag/retriever.build_production_retriever()` used by `api/server.py` **and** by every harness, so a harness cannot construct a different retriever than production without saying so explicitly. Harnesses that deliberately vary a parameter would pass an explicit override, making the variation **visible in the diff** instead of implicit in a default. This makes divergence **structurally impossible** rather than repeatedly caught by luck. It touches nine scripts plus the server wiring and belongs in its own baton. **A second, cheaper half:** the same helper should own the `retrieve()` argument defaults, since surface (2) shows construction parity alone is not enough.

- [HONESTY] **[P2 · TFDA data staleness — surfaced by the fly-214 citation gate 2026-07-29] The TFDA indication corpus is built from a PINNED snapshot and cites licences that EXPIRE; 有效日期 was DROPPED at build so no runtime staleness check is possible. The DEEP-LINK anchor decays FASTER than the document**
  - **The clock:** the first TFDA citation ever browser-eyeballed carries 有效日期 **116-01-18 = 2027-01-18** — under six months out. id=37 (未註銷藥品許可證資料集) is *active as of the snapshot* (`data/tfda/snapshot_20260630/`, pulled 2026-06-30). **Licences expire; a pinned snapshot does not.** This is an accuracy/honesty defect that **arrives on a schedule rather than from a code change** — no commit will ever cause it and no regression test will ever catch it.
  - **Measured** (all 10,941 docs joined to the snapshot on 許可證字號; **17,881 distinct cited licences, 0 unmatched, 0 missing dates**; ref 2026-07-29): **71 (0.4%) ALREADY EXPIRED** · <3mo 298 · 3–6mo 533 · 6–12mo 1,245 → **cumulative 2.1% at 3 months, 5.0% at 6, 12.0% at 12, 36.3% at 24.** By document (stale only when **all** its licences are): **34/10,941 (0.31%)** today; forward decay 1.5% / 3.7% / 8.9% at 3 / 6 / 12 months.
  - **⚠️ SUB-FINDING — representative-licence selection is not expiry-aware, so the link rots first.** `source_id` + `url` are built from ONE representative licence: **40 docs (0.4%) already deep-link to an expired licence; 476 (4.4%) to one expiring within 6 months.** **1,223 docs (11.2%) chose a representative that expires SOONER than one of its own siblings.** **8,748 (80.0%)** have only one licence, so no sibling exists. **The gate's own citation is an instance:** 冠脂妥膜衣錠5毫克 has **12** licences spanning 2027-01-18 → **2031-07-18**, and the deep-link anchors on **the earliest-expiring of the twelve**.
  - **有效日期 is NOT in the corpus.** Fields are `content · source_type · source_id · title · url · credibility · doc_type · drug_name · licenses · product_count`; **0/10,941** documents mention 有效日期 in content. **Therefore no runtime staleness check is currently possible** — the date is only recoverable by re-joining the snapshot ZIP.
  - **The build filtered once, and that filter is already decaying:** `_meta.stats` = `dropped_expired_or_no_date: 1069` at `ref_date: 2026/06/30`. **71 licences valid at build have expired in the 29 days since.**
  - **🔧 SCOPED 2026-07-29 — the cheap independent win, ready to fold into whatever ships next: an expiry-aware representative-licence SORT KEY.**
    - **What:** in `scripts/build_tfda_indication_corpus.py`, when a document's licence group has more than one 許可證字號, pick the representative by **latest 有效日期** instead of the current selection. **One sort key.**
    - **Why it is worth doing on its own:** **40 documents already deep-link to an EXPIRED licence** and **1,223 (11.2%) picked a representative expiring sooner than one of their own siblings**. The founder's own gate citation is an instance — **冠脂妥膜衣錠5毫克 has 12 licences spanning 2027-01-18 → 2031-07-18 and the link anchors on the earliest of the twelve.** Fixing the key buys ~4 extra years of link life on that document alone.
    - **Bounded by construction:** **8,748 documents (80.0%) have exactly one licence**, so nothing changes for four fifths of the corpus — the change touches only the 20% with a choice, and can only move a representative to a *later*-expiring one.
    - **⚠️ Cost gate — check before scoping further:** changing the representative changes `source_id`, `url` and possibly `title`. **If content stays byte-identical, NO re-embed is needed** — v196 already proved that path (*"corpus content byte-identical → NO re-embed"*). **If the representative name is interpolated into `content`, it is a re-embed.** Determine which before estimating. Cross-ref the v197 rep-name/rep-字號 mismatch fix, which is the same surface.
    - **NOT built here.**
  - **PROPOSED (not scoped):** id=37 is a **single anonymous bulk-ZIP GET** (OGDL v1.0, ~4.4 MB) and the manifest already says `"Pin + monthly re-pull"` with `pulled_at: 2026-06-30` — **⏰ SO THE FIRST MONTHLY RE-PULL IS DUE NOW (as of 2026-07-29, 29 days elapsed). This is an OPERATIONAL action for the founder, not a code task** — the pull is one anonymous GET; the only question is whether to re-embed (see the cost gate above). Open questions are cadence (monthly outruns a 2.1%/quarter decay) and whether a diff should gate anything — **the expensive part is the re-embed, not the pull**, and v196 proved a content-byte-identical refresh path exists (deep-link ship, no re-embed). **Cheapest independent win, orthogonal to cadence: make representative-licence selection prefer the LATEST-expiring licence** (one sort key in the build script; immediately fixes the 11.2%). Carrying 有效日期 into the record would enable a runtime check — a schema change, not free.
  - **Cross-ref [ADR 007](docs/decisions/007-tfda-open-data-grounding.md)** and the 在地差異 **(e)** item *"the authority URLs need periodic review"* (BACKLOG) — **same family, different data layer**; they should probably share one "external data goes stale on a schedule" mechanism rather than each growing its own cron.
  - **Copyright note (recorded so it is not re-litigated):** the `mcp.fda.gov.tw` page carries a site-content copyright notice, which is **distinct** from the corpus's provenance — the corpus is the **OGDL v1.0 open data (id=37)**, and **deep-linking is not reproduction**. Consistent with, and independent support for, the existing **a2 DEFER** reasoning. **No action needed.**
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-07-29. **Evidence:** [`docs/citation_gate_findings_20260729.md`](docs/citation_gate_findings_20260729.md) Finding 6. **NOT fixed — measurement only.**
